| `final class PersistenceManager { static let shared: PersistenceManager }` | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Persistence/PersistenceManager.swift:4-12 | NOT @MainActor, NOT an ObservableObject, NOT Sendable-annotated. Every method does synchronous blocking file I/O on the calling thread. Callable from anywhere, currently called from view bodies, view inits, actor-free singletons, and App.init(). |
| `func saveRoutes(_ routes: [SavedRoute]) / func loadRoutes() -> [SavedRoute]` | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Persistence/PersistenceManager.swift:18-24 | Whole-array replace of Documents/routes.json (atomic write). loadRoutes returns [] on any failure — a corrupt file is indistinguishable from an empty one. |
| `func saveScenarios(_ scenarios: [Scenario]) / func loadScenarios() -> [Scenario]` | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Persistence/PersistenceManager.swift:30-36 | Whole-array replace of Documents/scenarios.json. Same silent-empty-on-failure behaviour. |
| `func saveRoutineSchedules(_ schedules: [RoutineSchedule]) / func loadRoutineSchedules() -> [RoutineSchedule]` | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Persistence/PersistenceManager.swift:42-48 | Whole-array replace of Documents/routines.json. RoutineSchedule contains a Set<Int> of weekdays which encodes as a JSON array. |
| `func saveBookmarks(_ bookmarks: [LocationBookmark]) / func loadBookmarks() -> [LocationBookmark]` | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Persistence/PersistenceManager.swift:54-60 | Whole-array replace of Documents/bookmarks.json. MigrationManager also writes here (:48), so a migration can clobber existing bookmarks. |
| `func saveHistory(_ records: [SimulationRecord]) / func loadHistory() -> [SimulationRecord]` | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Persistence/PersistenceManager.swift:66-72 | Whole-array replace of Documents/history.json. SimulationRecord carries optional replayData ([TimestampedCoordinate], up to 5000 samples each) so this file grows large — 500 records × 5000 points is a multi-MB synchronous encode. |
| `func appendHistory(_ record: SimulationRecord)` | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Persistence/PersistenceManager.swift:74-82 | load → insert at index 0 → trim to 500 → save. Dead code; HistoryManager duplicates this inline at :82-84. |
| `struct PersistenceManager.AppSettings: Codable (noiseConfig, defaultTravelMode, deviceUpdateRateHz, simulationTickRateHz, mapFollowMode, map3DEnabled, mapStyle, trailEnabled, trailMaxPoints, autoReconnect, heartbeatIntervalSeconds, backgroundKeepAlive, appearance, showDeveloperInfo)` | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Persistence/PersistenceManager.swift:86-101 | All 14 fields have defaults, so decoding an older blob succeeds and new fields get their defaults — additive schema changes are safe without a migration bump. simulationTickRateHz (:90) and trailMaxPoints (:95) have no UI at all. |
| `func saveSettings(_ settings: AppSettings) / func loadSettings() -> AppSettings` | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Persistence/PersistenceManager.swift:105-116 | JSON-encodes into UserDefaults key "autospoof_settings_v2". loadSettings returns a fresh default AppSettings() on any decode failure — silent reset, no error surfaced. |
| `func exportGPX(coordinates: [CoordinateCodable], name: String) -> URL?` | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Persistence/PersistenceManager.swift:120-132 | Writes a GPX 1.1 trk/trkseg with lat/lon only (no elevation, no time). Returns the Documents URL, but returns it even if `try?` write failed — the non-nil URL is not proof the file exists. `name` is interpolated straight into the filename with no sanitising. |
| `func importGPX(from url: URL) -> [CoordinateCodable]?` | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Persistence/PersistenceManager.swift:134-156 | Regex #"lat=\"([^\"]+)\"\\s+lon=\"([^\"]+)\""# over the whole file; validates ranges; returns nil when zero points parse. Requires lat to precede lon with whitespace between — GPX files written lon-first will parse as empty. No security-scoped resource handling, so it will fail on a document-picker URL unless the caller calls startAccessingSecurityScopedResource. |
| `struct MigrationManager { static let currentVersion = 2; static func runIfNeeded() }` | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Persistence/MigrationManager.swift:5-24 | Idempotent by version stamp. Runs synchronously on the main thread from App.init(). Bump currentVersion and add an `if stored < N` block to add a step; note that a FRESH install (stored == 0) runs every historical step. |

---

## 5. Phan xu tung file

| File | Ket luan | Ly do |
|---|---|---|
| AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Views/MainViewV2.swift | **replace** | The screen is a single 292-line body mixing map rendering, camera policy, trail sampling, 12 sheet flags, an alert, and a 10-button icon stack with no accessibility labels and no view model. It also carries real defects (unreachable RoutineStudio sheet, latitude-only onChange, never-cleared trail, unused @StateObject on DeviceManager, tap-to-hijack with no confirmation). Rebuild it around an extracted MainViewModel, but port the feature list above one-for-one — especially the 10 map-button destinations and the tap-to-set-location proxy conversion. |
| AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Views/DashboardComponents.swift | **restyle** | The logic here is sound and largely presentational. TelemetryPanel's state gating (canPause/canResume) and the conditional metrics are correct and should be kept as behaviour; only the visual treatment needs work. TrailOverlay.sampleTrail is a pure function — reuse verbatim. ConnectionHeader and QuickActionsBar are thin enough to restyle in place. The one real change needed is moving formatDistance/formatDuration (duplicated between TelemetryPanel and TelemetryDetailView with different precision) into one shared formatter. |
| AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Views/OnboardingViewV2.swift | **restyle** | Structure is fine and it is the only screen that already respects Reduce Motion via safeAnimation. Keep the 4-step model, the @AppStorage key, and the pairing wiring exactly. Worth adding during the restyle: a cancel button for in-flight pairing (SelfPairingManager.cancel() exists and is unused) and surfacing the .failed(String) reason. Do not turn the free-swiping TabView into a locked stepper without deciding that deliberately — today users can swipe past pairing. |
| AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Views/SettingsView.swift | **replace** | The layout is a conventional Form and could be restyled, but the substance is broken: 13 of 14 persisted fields have no consumer, two status indicators are read from unobserved singletons, the appearance picker does nothing, and there is no unsaved-changes guard or reset-to-defaults. A redesign that only restyles will preserve a screen where most controls are placebo. Rebuild it and, per control, decide explicitly: wire it up, or remove it. Preserve without question the noise picker → coordinator.noiseConfig push on Save, the bundle-derived version string, and both bypass NavigationLinks. |
| AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Coordinator/SimulationCoordinator.swift | **engine-keep** | UI-agnostic enough to survive a rewrite: it imports only Combine/CoreLocation/Foundation, exposes a clean @Published surface plus imperative methods, and holds no view types. Two caveats for the new UI layer — currentCoordinate and noiseConfig are publicly settable (SettingsView already writes one directly, so the arbitration path can be bypassed), and startSession() has the non-obvious side effect of calling ShadowrocketManager.ensureVPNActive(), which can navigate the user out of the app. |
| AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Persistence/PersistenceManager.swift | **engine-refactor** | loadSettings/saveSettings are fine mechanically, but AppSettings is a single UserDefaults JSON blob whose decode failure silently returns defaults — a redesign that changes the struct will wipe user settings with no migration path (MigrationManager.runIfNeeded exists at AutoSpoofVNApp.swift:27 and should be extended to cover it). Also drop or implement simulationTickRateHz and trailMaxPoints, which have no UI at all. |
| AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Design/AppDesign.swift | **restyle** | The token set (AppColor/AppSpacing/AppFont/AppRadius) is the right seam for a visual redesign — change the token values, not the call sites. StatusBadge and MetricView are used throughout this cluster and their initializer signatures should stay stable. AppCard, EmptyStateView and ErrorBanner already exist but are unused here; the redesign can adopt them rather than inventing new ones. L10n is defined but only TelemetryPanel uses it — everything else is hardcoded Vietnamese. |
| AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Views/RouteStudioView.swift | **replace** | The view layer, the view model and the MKMapView bridge all need surgery, not paint: the bottom 'sheet' is a hand-rolled VStack overlay with no drag detents, tap-anywhere adds waypoints, the camera re-fits on every update, intermediate waypoints are dropped by calculateRoute(), Import GPX is dead, Export GPX discards its URL, saved routes lose their geometry, and startSimulation() leaks the simulation by not retaining RouteSimulator. Keep the 17 catalogued actions and the RouteProvider/RouteSimulator/PersistenceManager call signatures; rewrite everything around them. LocationSearchView (same file, lines 339-432) can be lifted out and restyled rather than replaced — but its nested SearchResult type must survive because AddBookmarkView depends on it. |
| AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Views/ScenarioStudioView.swift | **replace** | ScenarioEngine underneath is sound and UI-agnostic, but the view is the thinnest possible shell over it: hardcoded step parameters with no editors, five ScenarioAction cases unreachable, reordering wired but unusable without an EditButton, the pause control hides the resume path, the running panel is unreachable when the list is empty or a session is active, and the sheet uses the stale isPresented+optional pattern. A new step-editor UI is the single biggest feature win in this cluster; the run/pause/stop/save/delete API calls port over unchanged. |
| AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Views/BookmarksView.swift | **replace** | Structure (chips + searchable list + add sheet) is right and worth keeping conceptually, but the delete path is index-unsafe under filtering (real data-loss bug), state is a disk-seeded @State array with no shared store, the address captured during search is thrown away so a whole row line is dead, and invalid coordinate entry fails silently. Rewrite with an observable bookmark store and id-based mutations; keep setManualLocation, the 5 categories, and the search/manual-entry duality. CategoryChip (lines 111-130) is reusable as-is or restyle. |
| AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Views/HistoryView.swift | **restyle** | The cleanest file in the cluster: correct id-based deletion, sensible day grouping, and a replay panel that maps 1:1 onto ReplayEngine. It needs a destructive-action confirmation, exposure of the unused ReplayEngine.pause/resume (and a fixed seek before any scrubber), an isRecording indicator, resolution of the sheet-vs-NavigationLink double entry point (drop the internal NavigationStack or split into a content view + two wrappers), and accessibility labels — but the layout and wiring survive a restyle. |
| AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/Scenario/ScenarioEngine.swift | **engine-keep** | UI-agnostic: pure @MainActor ObservableObject that talks only to SimulationCoordinator, MotionEngine and PersistenceManager. Publishes everything a new UI needs. Only gap for a rewrite: acquire() failures are swallowed instead of published as an error — add a @Published lastError if the new UI should report contention. |
| AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/HistoryManager.swift | **engine-keep** | HistoryManager and ReplayEngine are both UI-agnostic and safe to keep. Two caveats for the redesign: the recording lifecycle lives in AutoSpoofVNApp's Combine sink (not in any view), so changing coordinator state transitions breaks recording; and ReplayEngine.seek(to:) only moves the progress bar without moving the replay cursor, so it must be implemented before a scrubber is exposed. |
| AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/Route/RouteSimulator.swift | **engine-refactor** | The class itself is UI-agnostic and richly instrumented (progress, distanceTravelled, distanceRemaining, eta, seek) — but nothing owns an instance. It must become a coordinator-owned or app-level object (or be driven through SimulationCoordinator) so the new UI can start a route, observe progress and pause/stop it; today its published state is invisible and its only call site leaks it. |
| AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Views/WorldTravelViewV2.swift | **restyle** | Domain wiring is thin and correct (repository search/grouping + two FlightManager calls), so the plumbing survives a visual rewrite. But it needs behavioural fixes alongside the restyle: bind the World Odyssey row to isAutoWorldOdysseyEnabled instead of a fixed play icon, add a way to clear/swap the origin-destination selection, and recompute the preview's cruise/ETA from the real 800 km/h rather than the hardcoded 850. Before deleting or moving the file, relocate the MotionEngine.haversineDistanceSync extension (:249-259) — Models/FlightTypes.swift depends on it. |
| AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Views/FlightHUDView.swift | **replace** | Dead view with zero call sites, so nothing constrains its shape. Rebuild it as part of the new main-map overlay and, critically, WIRE IT IN — the time-warp menu (6 presets) and 'Huỷ bay' are the only UI for those two capabilities anywhere in the app. Drop or properly source the Heading metric, which reads a telemetry field the flight path never writes. |
| AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Views/RoutineStudioView.swift | **replace** | The screen is unreachable, its editable location rows are wired to a closure that is never invoked, and the schedules it persists are never consumed by any engine. Restyling would ship three broken promises. Rebuild it with a real entry point in the new navigation, an actual coordinate picker calling updateLocation(for:coordinate:), and either wire RoutineSchedule into RoutineManager or drop the editor and keep only the honest 24/7 toggle + default-schedule reference. |
| AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Views/DiagnosticsV2View.swift | **replace** | The data model behind it is sound and should be kept verbatim (the full field list is catalogued above), but the view layer needs rebuilding: move the four PersistenceManager decodes out of the body, bind SelfPairingManager through a property wrapper, make runSelfDiagnostic actually async so the spinner works, call testConnection() before showing latency, replace the raw CLAuthorizationStatus integer with a label, and give both exports a share sheet plus a result state. Keep every one of the ~31 displayed fields and both export formats. |
| AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/FlightManager.swift | **engine-refactor** | Not a UI file, but this cluster cannot be redesigned safely without touching it. It is a god object (~500 lines, three raw Timers, hardcoded airport and destination catalogues duplicating AirportRepository) that writes GPS through SpoofEngine directly, bypassing SimulationCoordinator, and mutates RoutineManager's published state. It is NOT @MainActor. The redesign should at minimum route flight coordinate submission through SimulationCoordinator.submit(coordinate:from:speedKmh:headingDegrees:) so telemetry (especially heading) becomes real. |
| AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/RoutineManager.swift | **engine-refactor** | UI-agnostic enough to survive a rewrite (clean @Published surface, toggleAutoRoutine/updateLocation are usable as-is), but updateLocation's stringly-typed "home"/"work"/"cafe" parameter should become an enum, and evaluateCurrentTimeSchedule should consume the persisted RoutineSchedule list if that feature is to be honoured. Like FlightManager it bypasses SimulationCoordinator and writes to SpoofEngine directly. |
| AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/AirportRepository.swift | **engine-keep** | Fully UI-agnostic: a pure struct singleton with search/find/byCountry/vietnamese and 38 hardcoded airports. Any new UI can call it unchanged. Only note is that FlightManager.popularAirports duplicates a 10-airport subset of the same data and should eventually be collapsed into this repository. |
| AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Coordinator/SimulationCoordinator.swift | **engine-keep** | The @MainActor published surface is exactly what a new UI needs, and every field Diagnostics shows is already exposed. Two caveats for the redesign: runDiagnostics() hardcodes the Live Activity row to .unknown (it performs no check), and telemetry.headingDegrees is only populated by submit(), which the flight and routine paths bypass. |
| AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Persistence/PersistenceManager.swift | **engine-keep** | Simple synchronous JSON store; the API is fine for a new UI provided callers stop invoking load* from inside SwiftUI view bodies. |
| AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Views/ShadowrocketSetupView.swift | **replace** | 727 lines carrying 28 distinct features, a hand-rolled tab bar, a hand-rolled progress ring and four private layout helpers duplicated from other screens (SectionLabel vs SectionHeader in LocationCloakGuideView, LiveMetric vs MetricView in AppDesign). The Server tab also duplicates its own EndpointCards as TestButtons. Rebuild it on the design system, but port the domain wiring verbatim: the 5 @AppStorage keys, both shadowrocket://install URL builders with .urlQueryAllowed encoding, the App-Prefs deep link, the CoordinateServer start/stop pair and the onAppear auto-start. Add the missing ShadowrocketManager.markModuleImported() call while rewriting. |
| AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Views/LocationCloakGuideView.swift | **restyle** | Structurally sound: 3 live CheckItems bound to real published state, 4 self-contained expandable IssueCards, a static tech table and one link. Only the chrome needs to change. Two things must be fixed while restyling: rename the file to BypassTroubleshootView.swift (no LocationCloakGuideView type exists), and fold its private SectionHeader/CheckItem/IssueCard/TechRow into the shared design system rather than leaving near-duplicates of ShadowrocketSetupView's SectionLabel. |
| AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Views/DeviceManagerView.swift | **replace** | Small (169 lines) but structurally wrong: generateDiagnostics() is called from the View body doing synchronous filesystem I/O per render; three bare Buttons share a List row (tap-target hazard around a destructive action); it exposes only 4 of the 11 diagnostic fields and none of iosVersion/lastUpdate/autoReconnect/reconnect(); and it duplicates the pairing-start action in two sections. Replace, hoisting diagnostics into an observed @Published property on DeviceManager. Keep InfoRow/DiagRow as design-system components since the file is their only definition site. |
| AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Views/ShadowrocketBanner.swift | **replace** | 94 lines, correct @ObservedObject usage, but it has a dead onOpenSetup parameter (the whole 'open the full guide' path is unreachable), no dismiss affordance, an unreachable 4th state, and four separate computed properties re-deriving the same state machine with subtly different conditions (iconColor uses isReady while the others use the three flags). Rewrite as a single enum-driven state so title/icon/color/button/action cannot drift, and actually invoke onOpenSetup from a tap on the banner body. |
| AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Design/AppDesign.swift | **restyle** | Split verdict inside one file. The four token enums (lines 6-66) must be RESTYLED in place — keep every identifier (543 call sites across 16 view files depend on the names) and swap only the values; this is the single highest-leverage file in the redesign. The five components (lines 71-245) should be REPLACED: AppCard needs parameters, StatusBadge has an icon-tint bug at line 86, MetricView/EmptyStateView have sound APIs worth keeping signature-compatible, and ErrorBanner has zero call sites. The L10n enum (lines 250-278) should be REPLACED by real localized lookups against the existing .strings keys — 15 of its 19 members are already dead. |
| AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Design/Accessibility.swift | **replace** | Three of four helpers (accessibleButton, AccessibleMetric, ScalableText) have zero call sites, so nothing is lost by rewriting them. The one live helper, safeAnimation, is implemented wrong — it reads the UIKit global instead of @Environment(\.accessibilityReduceMotion) so it does not react to the setting changing. The redesign should rebuild this as an environment-driven set of modifiers and then actually apply them, since the app currently has no VoiceOver labels on icon-only buttons and no Dynamic Type clamping at all. |
| AutoSpoofVN/Resources/Info.plist | **engine-keep** | Load-bearing configuration, not UI. Every key except two is required by live code paths (Live Activities, audio+location background modes, three location/local-network usage strings, two of three Bonjour service types, the shadowrocket URL scheme). Preserve it wholesale. Only two edits are justified: drop BGTaskSchedulerPermittedIdentifiers (registerBackgroundTask() is an empty stub) and, if a branded splash is wanted, populate the currently empty UILaunchScreen dict. Revisit UISupportedInterfaceOrientations only if the new design needs landscape. |
| AutoSpoofVN/Resources/AutoSpoofVN.entitlements | **engine-refactor** | Content is correct for Debug but the file is wired at target base level in project.yml:88, so Release archives inherit get-task-allow=true and will be rejected by App Store submission. Needs splitting into per-config entitlements (or a Release variant without the key) before any shipping build. |
| AutoSpoofVN/Resources/en.lproj/Localizable.strings | **engine-refactor** | The 66 keys are well-organised and complete but load zero times at runtime. Keep the key set — it is a free, already-structured copy deck for the new UI — but the refactor is on the Swift side: every hardcoded literal in the 16 view files has to become a lookup, and the file needs the missing keys the views currently hardcode inline. |
| AutoSpoofVN/Resources/vi.lproj/Localizable.strings | **engine-refactor** | Same as en.lproj — a complete Vietnamese mirror with identical keys, currently unreferenced. It already contains correct strings the code gets wrong (e.g. "empty.scenarios" which ScenarioStudioView.swift:17 substitutes with the routes string), so wiring it up fixes existing copy bugs for free. |
| project.yml | **engine-refactor** | Structurally sound (two schemes cleanly separate the mock build from the real-FFI build; the project-wide MARKETING_VERSION/CURRENT_PROJECT_VERSION is deliberate so the widget extension version always matches). A redesign forces three additions: an Assets.xcassets source entry plus ASSETCATALOG_COMPILER_APPICON_NAME and GLOBAL_ACCENT_COLOR_NAME, a per-config CODE_SIGN_ENTITLEMENTS override for Release, and TARGETED_DEVICE_FAMILY/orientation revisits if the new layout wants landscape. |
| AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/RouteProvider.swift | **engine-keep** | Genuinely UI-agnostic: pure actor, no SwiftUI import, no coordinator reference, returns plain DTOs. It survives any UI rewrite untouched. The only items worth attention are the unstructured-Task cancellation gap and the fact that three public APIs (diagnosticsSnapshot, resetDiagnostics, clearCache) plus fetchRoutePlan have zero call sites — keep them, they are latent features for a diagnostics screen, not dead code to prune. |
| AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/Route/RouteSimulator.swift | **engine-refactor** | Not UI-agnostic enough to survive as-is. It is a @MainActor ObservableObject with no owner (the current call site leaks it, so the route feature does not actually run), it reaches SimulationCoordinator.shared directly, pause() behaves as stop(), it recomputes O(n) route distance three-plus times per 10 Hz tick on the main actor, and it ignores submit()'s failure return. The redesign must give it a real owner (@StateObject or a shared manager), precompute cumulative distances, fix pause, and honour the submit result. Its six @Published values are the right HUD contract to keep. |
| AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/Scenario/ScenarioEngine.swift | **engine-refactor** | Mostly UI-agnostic (no SwiftUI beyond ObservableObject) and the CRUD surface is clean, but it hard-wires SimulationCoordinator.shared and PersistenceManager.shared, exposes `scenarios` as a mutable @Published var that bypasses persistence, has no error channel for a failed acquire, releases the GPS source on pause, and never opens a SimulationSession (so scenario runs produce no telemetry/history unlike route runs). Five of its eleven actions have no UI, one (followRoute) is dead against a field nobody writes, and two silently ignore their associated values. Keep the execution model, fix the coupling and the action gaps. |
| AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/Motion/MotionEngine.swift | **engine-keep** | Fully UI-agnostic and reusable; the phase machine and the four geodesic statics are the app's shared math and are unit-tested. Two caveats for the rewrite: making the pure statics `static` on an actor forces awaits everywhere (which already spawned the duplicated haversineDistanceSync in a View file — worth hoisting into a free enum in Models), and deceleration floors at speedProfile.min so speed never reaches zero on arrival. |
| AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/Motion/HeadingEngine.swift | **engine-keep** | Small, pure, value-type, no UI dependency, unit-tested (SimulationTests.swift:169). cardinalDirection is a ready-made localized compass label with zero call sites — free win for the new UI. Only structural caution: being a struct, it must stay in a mutable stored property. |
| AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Views/RouteStudioView.swift | **replace** | Read only as the cluster's call site. Its startSimulation() is where the RouteSimulator ownership bug lives (local `let`, immediately deallocated) and its saveRoute() is where routeGeometry fails to be persisted, killing ScenarioAction.followRoute. Both must be fixed as part of any rewrite of this screen, not carried over. |
| AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Views/ScenarioStudioView.swift | **replace** | Read only as the cluster's call site. The engine supports more than the view exposes: no resume button despite ScenarioEngine.resume() existing, no menu items for followRoute/changeTravelMode/pause/resume/loop, every add-step action inserts hardcoded constants with no editor, and stepDetail renders a loop count the engine ignores. A rewrite should close these gaps rather than reproduce the current subset. |
| AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/FlightManager.swift | **engine-refactor** | Logic bay (great-circle, phase, tour) hoàn toàn UI-agnostic và đáng giữ, NHƯNG file này không phải engine thuần: nó ghi trực tiếp vào RoutineManager.shared (7 thuộc tính) và SpoofEngine.shared, sở hữu 3 Timer ở RunLoop .default, và trộn dữ liệu (14 sân bay + 8 điểm đến hardcode) với hành vi. Trước khi thay UI cần: (1) tách dữ liệu destination ra repository giống AirportRepository, (2) đưa timer sang .common hoặc Task, (3) bọc lại việc ghi trạng thái sang RoutineManager thành một API rõ ràng, (4) sửa toggleAutoWorldOdyssey(false) để thực sự dừng bay. Không cần viết lại từ đầu. |
| AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/RoutineManager.swift | **engine-refactor** | Phần mô phỏng chuyển động (đi bộ liên tục, ramp tốc độ, đèn đỏ, đổi sang đường thật MKDirections) chất lượng cao, có chú thích lý do rõ ràng và UI-agnostic — giữ nguyên. Cần refactor: updateLocation stringly-typed nên đổi thành enum, routineTimer chuyển sang RunLoop .common, cờ isTravelOverrideActive phải persist để tránh mất địa điểm thật, và cần nối RoutineSchedule đã lưu vào evaluateCurrentTimeSchedule(). Không @MainActor dù mutate @Published — nên gắn @MainActor khi có dịp. |
| AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/AirportRepository.swift | **engine-keep** | Đúng chuẩn: struct value-type, dữ liệu thuần, 3 hàm truy vấn không phụ thuộc UI, không timer, không singleton mutable. Redesign chỉ cần gọi lại search/byCountry/vietnamese. Ghi chú duy nhất: byCountry nên cache nếu danh sách lớn hơn, và find(code:) hiện chưa có caller. |
| AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/HistoryManager.swift | **engine-refactor** | HistoryManager + ReplayEngine đều @MainActor và giao tiếp qua SimulationCoordinator nên sống sót được một bản UI mới, nhưng có ba khiếm khuyết phải sửa cùng lúc: tham số của startRecording bị bỏ qua khiến mọi record gán sai nguồn, I/O file JSON đồng bộ trên main thread, và cặp pause()/resume() + seek() của ReplayEngine hỏng/stub. Nên tách ReplayEngine ra file riêng khi refactor. |
| AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/Device/DeviceManager.swift | **engine-refactor** | UI-agnostic in shape (no SwiftUI import, pure @Published facade) so it survives a rewrite, but it owns a duplicate copy of device state that competes with SimulationCoordinator.deviceState, hardcodes transportType, never fills iosVersion, and exposes autoReconnect/heartbeat interval that the Settings screen only pretends to control. Unify the device-state source of truth and wire the two orphaned settings before rebuilding the UI on top. |
| AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/Device/ConnectionRecovery.swift | **engine-refactor** | Correct concept and fully UI-agnostic, but it publishes isRecovering/retryCount/networkAvailable that no view consumes, its cancelRecovery() has no caller, and its retry Task mutates main-actor state without an explicit hop. The redesign should surface recovery progress and add a cancel affordance rather than leave a 62s silent stall. |
| AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/DeviceTransport.swift | **engine-refactor** | Dead code — zero references anywhere in app, widgets or tests. Either adopt it (route SpoofEngine's inline FFI through DVTDeviceTransport, which would finally make MockDeviceTransport useful for tests and give the UI a real transport label) or delete it. Leaving it invites a redesign to wire the new UI to an abstraction that is not actually in the data path. |
| AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/SelfPairingManager.swift | **engine-keep** | The pairing state machine is the app's most feature-dense engine and its Phase enum maps cleanly onto any new UI. Keep it as-is for the redesign; the defects (empty registerBackgroundTask, always-true permission probe, cancel() not aborting the FFI host, unused .connectingTunnel case) are behavioural fixes, not reasons to restructure, and its published surface needs no change. |

| AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/ShadowrocketManager.swift | **engine-refactor** | UI-agnostic and its five-state statusMessage ladder is exactly what a banner needs, but it duplicates the module-imported flag that ShadowrocketSetupView tracks separately, infers VPN state from two contradictory heuristics, runs an un-invalidatable 5s poll, exposes an unreachable resetSetup(), and lets startSession switch apps. Reconcile the two setup-state stores before rebuilding the banner. |

Also wire an actual off-switch so the Settings toggle stops being decorative. |
| D:/ToolGN/AutoSpoofVN/AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/LiveActivityManager.swift | **engine-refactor** | Correct layer (no SwiftUI dependency) and the ContentState contract with the widget target is clean, but it hard-wires six singletons in init and has an unreachable endActivity(). Refactor to take its inputs from SimulationCoordinator alone (or an explicit snapshot struct), call endActivity() on stop/complete, clear currentActivity when the activity's state becomes .dismissed/.ended, and drop the 10 Hz coordinate publisher from the update stream. |
| D:/ToolGN/AutoSpoofVN/AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/AppRecoveryManager.swift | **engine-keep** | Small, @MainActor, UI-agnostic, and depends only on SimulationCoordinator's public API. It survives a UI rewrite unchanged; only its two alert buttons and the willResignActive subscription need re-attaching at the new root. Optional improvement (not required): consume the travelMode/lastSpeed/elapsedSeconds/routeName it already persists. |
| D:/ToolGN/AutoSpoofVN/AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/TimerAudit.swift | **engine-refactor** | Dead code with a stale, self-contradicting doc-comment. Either delete it or turn it into a real data source for the redesigned Diagnostics screen (make Entry Identifiable, derive isRunning from actual timer handles rather than proxy booleans, and cover all 13 owners the comment claims). Do not ship the current comment as documentation. |
| D:/ToolGN/AutoSpoofVN/AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/DeterministicRandom.swift | **engine-keep** | Correct, self-contained value type with no dependencies and no consumers — a UI rewrite cannot break it. Keep as-is (or delete) but do not present 'deterministic simulation' as an existing feature in the redesign; nothing uses it. If it is ever adopted, fix the modulo bias and the mislabelled algorithm comment. |
| D:/ToolGN/AutoSpoofVN/AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/AppLogger.swift | **engine-keep** | 17 lines, zero coupling, used across the whole app. Keep verbatim; new UI code should log through AppLogger.ui. | D:/ToolGN/AutoSpoofVN/AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Views/DiagnosticsV2View.swift | **replace** | 206-line List-of-Sections that is the sole surface for this cluster's state. It mixes presentation with logic (two diagnostic rows appended in the view, two file-export implementations inline), double-presents itself as both sheet and pushed view, and shows a raw CLAuthorizationStatus integer. Rebuild it, but re-wire all 5 background fields, the self-diagnostic button plus its two view-local rows, and both export actions. |
| D:/ToolGN/AutoSpoofVN/AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Views/SettingsView.swift | **restyle** | Only the 'Chạy nền' section belongs to this cluster (SettingsView.swift:79-92). | D:/ToolGN/AutoSpoofVN/AutoSpoofVN/Sources/AutoSpoofVNApp.swift | **restyle** | The root scene is where every lifecycle hook in this cluster lives (bootstrap, recovery alert, willResignActive checkpoint, willEnterForeground reconnect). Keep the file's structure and re-attach all four modifiers verbatim to whatever new root container replaces the anonymous Group; this is the single highest-risk file for silent feature loss. |
| D:/ToolGN/AutoSpoofVN/AutoSpoofWidgets/AutoSpoofWidgetsBundle.swift | **reuse** | Separate widget-extension target rendered by the system, not part of the app UI rewrite. | AutoSpoofWidgets/AutoSpoofWidgetsBundle.swift | **restyle** | The structure is correct and complete for a Live Activity — all four Dynamic Island presentations plus the Lock Screen are implemented and read only from ContentState, so the file has no coupling to app internals. It needs a visual pass (hardcoded .green/.red/.blue everywhere, no design tokens, no dark/light differentiation, no accessibility labels) and two logic fixes (the flightProgress/flightNumber asymmetry at :70 vs :142, and the '45k' speed format at :162). Do NOT replace it — rewriting risks losing one of the six presentation variants, and no test would catch that. |
| AutoSpoofWidgets/Info.plist | **reuse** | Minimal and correct: widgetkit-extension point, XPC! package type, version strings inherited from project.yml so app and extension can never drift. Only touch it if the redesign adds frequent updates (NSSupportsLiveActivitiesFrequentUpdates) or a Home Screen/Control widget. |
| AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Models/ActivityAttributes.swift | **engine-keep** | 24 lines, imports only ActivityKit and Foundation, compiled into both targets. This is exactly the right shape for an app↔widget contract. Treat additions as breaking: an Activity started by the old binary decodes against the old ContentState. |
| AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/LiveActivityManager.swift | **engine-refactor** | Functionally UI-agnostic (it builds a value type and hands it to ActivityKit), but it hard-references four singletons and re-implements source-priority arbitration in an if/else ladder at :42-60 that duplicates SimulationSource.priority. It also never calls its own endActivity(). Refactor to take a single injected state snapshot from SimulationCoordinator and wire endActivity to session stop — but keep the six-publisher merge and the 800ms throttle exactly as they are. |
| AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Persistence/PersistenceManager.swift | **engine-refactor** | The file/JSON layout, the 500-record history cap, and the GPX writer/parser are all sound and worth keeping verbatim — the redesign must not re-invent the on-disk format or existing installs lose data. What must change: make it an actor or move I/O off the calling thread, surface errors instead of swallowing them (:160-172), and add an observable settings store so multiple screens cannot hold divergent AppSettings copies. |
| AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Persistence/MigrationManager.swift | **engine-keep** | Keep the version-stamp mechanism and keep it running in App.init() before any view. Two of the three steps are effectively dead (v1→v2 routine locations is a literal no-op; the bookmarks step reads a key nothing in the tree writes) but do not delete them without checking a real device's UserDefaults — the whole point of the class is to serve installs older than this repo. Do add a merge instead of the wholesale saveBookmarks overwrite at :48. |
| AutoSpoofVNTests/SimulationTests.swift | **engine-refactor** | 22 tests, of which 21 assert correct behaviour and 1 (testSmoothHeadingNoJump, :45-49) is provably wrong and fails today. testZeroDistanceRoute (:227) is vacuous. Everything else is a genuine regression net for the model layer and MUST survive the redesign — if the new UI keeps SimulationState, SimulationSource, GPSNoiseConfig and AppSettings, these tests keep protecting them unchanged. |
| AutoSpoofVNTests/EngineTests.swift | **engine-keep** | The strongest file in the suite: each class documents the real production bug it was written against (WAV header fields left blank causing AVAudioPlayer error 1685348671; source arbitration; great-circle vs linear interpolation; the `!= 0.0` sentinel that discarded coordinate (0,0)). No UI coupling at all. These tests are the regression net for the legacy engine the redesign will be bridging to — keep every one. |
| AutoSpoofVNTests/RouteProviderTests.swift | **engine-keep** | Four tight tests over RouteProvider.resample/straightLineCoordinates and the RoutePlan extension, covering exact endpoint preservation, even spacing, and the point-count cap. Fully UI-agnostic; nothing here needs to change for a redesign. |
| project.yml | **engine-refactor** | Targets and schemes are sound, and the Mock-vs-RealFFI split via SWIFT_ACTIVE_COMPILATION_CONDITIONS (:105-108) is the right shape. Two gaps a redesign will hit: AutoSpoofWidgets has no entitlements and no App Group (blocking any widget that needs app data), and AutoSpoofVNTests covers only the app target so the widget is untestable. |

| medium | Trail state is never cleared. `trail` is local @State that only grows/decimates; stopSession() resets telemetry but nothing resets the polyline, so the previous session's path stays drawn over the next one and across source switches. | MainViewV2.swift:31, 278-282 (no reset in the onStop closure at :84-87) |
| medium | Trail + camera updates are driven by latitude only. A movement that changes longitude without changing latitude (due-east travel, or any float-identical latitude tick) skips both the trail sample and the camera follow. | MainViewV2.swift:99-101 `.onChange(of: coordinator.currentCoordinate.latitude)` |
| medium | Main-thread cost per coordinate tick: every update appends to `trail`, triggers a full Map re-render with a MapPolyline of up to 1000 points, and runs a 0.3s withAnimation camera move. At high update rates this is the app's dominant main-thread load, and the periodic decimation reallocates the whole array. | MainViewV2.swift:278-291 + DashboardComponents.swift:216-222 |
| medium | @StateObject on shared singletons. MainViewV2 wraps SimulationCoordinator.shared, ShadowrocketManager.shared, DeviceManager.shared and HistoryManager.shared in @StateObject even though the app already injects the coordinator as @EnvironmentObject. @StateObject is meant to own a fresh instance; here it just subscribes. `deviceManager` is never referenced in the body at all, and `historyManager` is only used for a fire-and-forget call — both cause pointless full-view invalidations on every publish from those managers. | MainViewV2.swift:11-14 (deviceManager :13 unused; historyManager :14 used once at :86) |
| medium | ShadowrocketBanner's onOpenSetup closure is dead. | medium | noiseConfig.drift is persisted and pushed to the live coordinator, but applyNoise() reads only enabled/radiusMeters/inertia — the drift flag never affects the jitter model. | SettingsView.swift:32 → SimulationCoordinator.swift:220-246 |
| medium | SettingsView reads two ObservableObject singletons directly inside body without observing them, so both status indicators are frozen at whatever value existed when the Form was built. | SettingsView.swift:86-87 (BackgroundKeeper.shared.isAudioRunning), :113 (CoordinateServer.shared.isRunning) |
| low | Settings edits are lost silently. `settings` is a snapshot taken at view init; "Đóng" dismisses without saving and without warning about unsaved changes. There is also no reset-to-defaults action. | SettingsView.swift:5, 166-168 |
| medium | AppSettings is a single JSON blob under "autospoof_settings_v2"; a decode failure silently returns fresh defaults, so any Codable change made during the redesign wipes every user setting with no migration and no error. | PersistenceManager.swift:111-116 |
| medium | The telemetry-detail sheet is only reachable via an undiscoverable tap on the whole telemetry panel — no chevron, no button, no accessibility trait. It is the sole read-out for altitude, distance travelled, motion phase, noise radius and time multiplier. | MainViewV2.swift:92 |

It stores into AppBootstrap.shared.cancellables with no guard, and it is invoked from both .onAppear and .onChange; a second invocation adds a second $state sink that would start/stop history recording twice. | AutoSpoofVNApp.swift:44-51, 89-101 |

### Views: ShadowrocketSetup + LocationCloakGuide (BypassTroubleshoot) + DeviceManager + ShadowrocketBanner

| Muc | Rui ro | Vi tri |

| medium | Three Buttons sit side-by-side in an HStack inside a SwiftUI List row with no .buttonStyle(.borderless)/.plain ("Ngắt kết nối" / "Test" / "Xoá GPS"). In a List, the row swallows the tap and can fire the wrong button. "Xoá GPS" is destructive, so a mis-tap clears the device's simulated location. | DeviceManagerView.swift:35-46 |
| medium | DeviceManagerView is only reachable from QuickActionsBar, which MainViewV2 renders only when !coordinator.state.isActive (MainViewV2.swift:71). While a simulation is running or paused the user cannot open Device Manager — yet BypassTroubleshootView:60 explicitly tells them to "kết nối trong Device Manager" when the DVT check fails, which is exactly the running-session case. | MainViewV2.swift:71-77 vs LocationCloakGuideView.swift:56-61 |
| medium | The app has two parallel device-connection sources of truth: DeviceManager.connectionState (used by DeviceManagerView) and SimulationCoordinator.deviceState (used by BypassTroubleshootView, ConnectionHeader). Both derive from SpoofEngine but through separate Combine chains, so they can disagree transiently — a redesign that unifies the header risks showing a different state than the Device Manager sheet. | DeviceManager.swift:159-184 vs SimulationCoordinator.swift:24, 344-359 |
| medium | CoordinateServer.start() is called from at least four places (SimulationCoordinator.swift:57, ShadowrocketManager.swift:154 and :85, ShadowrocketSetupView.swift:79) and is guarded only by `guard !isRunning`, but isRunning is set asynchronously in the NWListener .ready handler. Two near-simultaneous starts can allocate two listeners on port 8765; allowLocalEndpointReuse hides the failure and the second listener silently loses. | CoordinateServer.swift:39-69, callers at SimulationCoordinator.swift:57, ShadowrocketManager.swift:85/:154, ShadowrocketSetupView.swift:79 |
| medium | ShadowrocketManager.checkVPNStatus() infers "VPN active" from ANY utun/ipsec/ppp network interface (:200-213). Any other VPN, a Personal Hotspot, or an iCloud Private Relay tunnel makes isVPNActive true, so the banner claims readiness and the auto-trigger stops nagging even though Shadowrocket is off. It is also driven by a repeating 5s Timer created in init and never invalidated except in deinit of a singleton (i.e. never). | ShadowrocketManager.swift:187-219, 251-253 |
| medium | ShadowrocketManager.importModule() starts CoordinateServer then immediately reads coordServer.isRunning to choose local vs GitHub (:85-93). Because isRunning only flips true asynchronously, the first tap almost always falls through to the STATIC GitHub module (fixed coordinates) rather than the live local one, silently degrading the feature. | ShadowrocketManager.swift:83-103 |
| medium | ShadowrocketManager.detectInstallation() runs only in init. Installing Shadowrocket from the banner's App Store button and returning to the app leaves isInstalled == false until relaunch, so the banner keeps saying "Cần Shadowrocket". No scenePhase/foreground re-detect and no manual refresh affordance exist. | ShadowrocketManager.swift:57-71 (no re-invocation anywhere) |

| low | "Dừng" stops the CoordinateServer that Shadowrocket depends on, with no confirmation and no warning that it will break an active bypass. Reopening the sheet silently restarts it via onAppear, so the state is confusing. | ShadowrocketSetupView.swift:311-313 vs :78-80 |

| low | All four views use `@StateObject private var x = SomeType.shared`. @StateObject is for owned objects; with a singleton the autoclosure still resolves to .shared so behaviour is correct today, but it signals ownership the view does not have and will fight any future migration to @Observable / injected environment objects. ShadowrocketBanner correctly uses @ObservedObject instead — the cluster is inconsistent. | ShadowrocketSetupView.swift:11-12, LocationCloakGuideView.swift:6-7, DeviceManagerView.swift:5-6 vs ShadowrocketBanner.swift:11 |

### Design layer + Resources + localization + Info.plist + entitlements

| medium | StatusBadge renders the icon in the WRONG colour. Inside the HStack the Image(systemName:) at line 86-87 gets only .font(.system(size: 8)) — no .foregroundStyle — while only the Text gets .foregroundStyle(color). The symbol therefore inherits the ambient label colour (black/white), sitting on a color.opacity(0.12) tinted capsule. This affects 8 of the 11 call sites (all the ones that pass an icon). A redesign that reproduces the component 'faithfully' will reproduce the bug. | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Design/AppDesign.swift:83-95 |
| medium | The Appearance setting is a dead control. SettingsView.swift:96-100 offers a system/light/dark Picker bound to $settings.appearance, PersistenceManager.AppSettings.appearance persists it, but nothing in the codebase ever calls .preferredColorScheme — grep for preferredColorScheme/colorScheme returns zero hits in AutoSpoofVN/Sources. Users can pick Dark and nothing happens. Redesign must either wire it at the WindowGroup root or remove the control. | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Views/SettingsView.swift:94-103, PersistenceManager.swift:99, AutoSpoofVNApp.swift:31-53 |
| medium | 543 token references (189 AppColor + 167 AppSpacing + 153 AppFont + 34 AppRadius) spread over 16 view files, concentrated in ShadowrocketSetupView (110 refs) and DashboardComponents/WorldTravelViewV2/OnboardingViewV2. Renaming or restructuring any token enum breaks all 16 files simultaneously with no incremental migration path. Keep the identifiers, swap the values. | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Design/AppDesign.swift:6-66 consumed across AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Views/*.swift |
| medium | safeAnimation reads UIAccessibility.isReduceMotionEnabled as a one-shot global inside the view body rather than using @Environment(\.accessibilityReduceMotion). Toggling Reduce Motion while the app is running does not invalidate the view, so the setting only takes effect on an unrelated re-render. It is also the only accessibility helper actually in use — the other three (accessibleButton, AccessibleMetric, ScalableText) have zero call sites, so the app has no VoiceOver labels on icon-only buttons and no Dynamic Type clamping anywhere. | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Design/Accessibility.swift:13-15 (used once at OnboardingViewV2.swift:29); dead helpers at Accessibility.swift:6-10, :19-29, :32-46 |

### Engines: RouteProvider + RouteSimulator + ScenarioEngine + MotionEngine + HeadingEngine

| Muc | Rui ro | Vi tri |
|---|---|---|
| medium | MotionEngine's geodesic helpers are `static` members of an ACTOR, so every pure math call is an await/suspension point. RouteSimulator's per-tick distance walks and ScenarioEngine.moveToward pay actor-hop cost for arithmetic. The codebase already worked around this by duplicating the haversine body as haversineDistanceSync inside a View file. Any redesign that adds more per-frame distance readouts multiplies the problem. | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/Motion/MotionEngine.swift:107-155 and AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Views/WorldTravelViewV2.swift:249-260 |
| medium | MotionEngine.haversineDistanceSync — a required dependency of Models/FlightTypes.swift:15 — is declared in an extension at the bottom of a SwiftUI View file. Deleting or splitting WorldTravelViewV2.swift during the redesign breaks the model layer's compilation in a place nobody will look. | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Views/WorldTravelViewV2.swift:248-260 |
| medium | RouteProvider's in-flight Task is unstructured, so outer cancellation never reaches it. `Task { try await Self.calculateRoute(...) }` (RouteProvider.swift:187-189) is detached from the caller's cancellation scope; the withTaskCancellationHandler/directions.cancel() inside calculateRoute can therefore never fire from a caller cancelling. RoutineManager.requestRealRoad relies on routeTask?.cancel() (RoutineManager.swift:325) to abandon stale legs — the MKDirections request keeps running and consumes quota. Additionally, a cancelled caller sets pendingRequests[key] = nil (line 205) while other callers are still awaiting the same task, so a later caller can spawn a duplicate request. | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/RouteProvider.swift:183-219 and 398-415 |
| medium | MotionEngine decelerates only to speedProfile.min, not to zero (`currentSpeedKmh = max(speedProfile.min, ...)`, line 50). For .driving min is 5 km/h, .cycling 5, .motorcycle 10 — the simulated device never actually comes to rest at a route end; it creeps until distanceToTarget drops under 5 m and the phase snaps to .stopped. Any UI that renders speed will show a non-zero speed while 'arriving'. | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/Motion/MotionEngine.swift:47-50 with AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Models/SimulationTypes.swift:134-144 |
| medium | Two competing heading computations. RouteSimulator discards result.headingDegrees (already smoothed at factor 0.15 inside MotionEngine, line 71) and instead re-derives a heading from the emitted coordinate through HeadingEngine (also factor 0.15) — double low-pass filtering plus a dependency on the noise-free pre-submit coordinate. ScenarioEngine uses result.headingDegrees directly. The two paths therefore produce different compass behaviour for the same motion; a unified redesign HUD will show inconsistent headings between route and scenario runs. | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/Route/RouteSimulator.swift:94-101 vs AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/Scenario/ScenarioEngine.swift:192-195 |
| medium | ScenarioEngine.start() fails silently on arbitration loss: `guard coordinator.acquire(.scenario) else { isRunning = false; return }` publishes nothing — no error string, no alert hook. The user taps play and nothing happens. The redesign needs an error surface (there is no @Published error property on ScenarioEngine to bind to). | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/Scenario/ScenarioEngine.swift:30-33 |
| medium | ScenarioEngine.stop() calls release(.scenario) but never stopSession(), so the coordinator session (and its device update timer, started by startSession) is left in whatever state it was in. Meanwhile ScenarioEngine.start() never calls startSession() at all — it only acquires. Scenario runs therefore have no SimulationSession record, no telemetry.elapsedTime baseline (sessionStartDate stays nil) and no history entry, unlike route runs. Feature parity between the two studios is broken today; the redesign should not assume scenario runs populate coordinator.session. | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/Scenario/ScenarioEngine.swift:24-49 vs AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/Route/RouteSimulator.swift:38 |
| medium | ScenarioEngine.scenarios is @Published var (not private(set)) while every other state field is private(set). A new UI can bind a List directly to it and mutate it, silently bypassing PersistenceManager and losing the user's scenarios on next launch. | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/Scenario/ScenarioEngine.swift:13 |
| medium | ScenarioAction.loop(times:) discards its associated value (`case .loop: break`), but ScenarioEditorView.stepDetail renders 'Lặp N lần' for it. Any UI that offers the step is telling the user a number the engine ignores. | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/Scenario/ScenarioEngine.swift:151-152 vs AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Views/ScenarioStudioView.swift:220 |
| medium | ScenarioAction.moveTo(_, speedKmh:) ignores its speedKmh argument — moveToward() never touches the SpeedProfile, so movement speed comes from whatever the last changeSpeed/changeTravelMode step (or the driving default) left in the MotionEngine. The editor lets you type a speed per moveTo step and it has no effect. | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/Scenario/ScenarioEngine.swift:119-120 and 177-199 |
| medium | ScenarioEngine.moveAlongRoute crashes on a single-point route: `for i in 0..<coords.count - 1` traps when coords.count == 0 (0..<(-1) is an invalid Range). RouteProvider can legitimately return a one-waypoint plan for a degenerate route (RouteProvider.swift:155-168), and a persisted routeGeometry of length 0/1 reaches this line unguarded. | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/Scenario/ScenarioEngine.swift:202 |
| medium | RouteSimulator.totalRouteDistance() has the same shape (`for i in 0..<routeCoordinates.count - 1`) but is protected by the count >= 2 guard in start(). If the redesign adds any other entry point (e.g. wiring seek() or restoring a saved route) that guard must be replicated or it traps. | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/Route/RouteSimulator.swift:27 guard vs 133 and 141 |
| medium | ScenarioEngine.pause() invoked from a `.pause` STEP busy-waits at 5 Hz inside executeAction with no exit other than isRunning flipping true — and the only thing that flips it is resume(), which has no UI button. A scenario containing a pause step is unstoppable except via the Dừng button (which cancels the Task). | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/Scenario/ScenarioEngine.swift:141-146 |
| medium | Singletons reached from engine internals rather than injected: ScenarioEngine and RouteSimulator both hard-wire SimulationCoordinator.shared (ScenarioEngine.swift:17, RouteSimulator.swift:20) and ScenarioEngine hard-wires PersistenceManager.shared (lines 21, 76, 81, 123). Not testable, not previewable, and a SwiftUI preview of any new scenario UI will hit the real coordinator (which in turn starts CoordinateServer and can trigger the Shadowrocket VPN via startSession). | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/Scenario/ScenarioEngine.swift:17,21 and AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/Route/RouteSimulator.swift:20 |
| medium | startSession() has an app-level side effect the engines do not advertise: it calls ShadowrocketManager.shared.ensureVPNActive() and starts a device update timer (SimulationCoordinator.swift:117-120). Any redesign that calls RouteSimulator.start() from a preview, an onAppear, or a speculative 'warm up' path will attempt to bring up the VPN. | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Coordinator/SimulationCoordinator.swift:112-120, reached from RouteSimulator.swift:38 |
| low | RouteProvider diagnostics counters are inaccurate: record() increments fallbackCount only when errorDescription != nil (line 379), so the legitimate degenerate-distance fallback (line 156) and any nil-error fallback update lastRouteSource without incrementing either counter. mapKitRouteCount + fallbackCount will not equal the number of resolveRoute calls. A diagnostics panel built on these numbers would mislead. | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/RouteProvider.swift:373-384 |
| low | RouteProvider cacheAccessOrder maintenance uses removeAll{} on an Array for every hit and store — O(n) per access with n up to 64. Harmless at this size but it is the kind of thing a redesign might scale up (e.g. a route history browser) without noticing. | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/RouteProvider.swift:354-370 |
| low | RouteProvider.clearCache() does not clear pendingRequests, so a 'clear cache' UI action would leave an in-flight request that then repopulates the cache immediately after the user cleared it. | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/RouteProvider.swift:248-251 |
| low | RouteSimulator.seek() moves currentIndex and submits a coordinate but leaves distanceTravelled untouched, so after a seek the published progress (recomputed from distanceTravelled on the next tick, line 105) snaps back to the pre-seek value. Wiring the ready-made scrubber into the new UI without fixing this produces a visibly fighting slider. | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/Route/RouteSimulator.swift:70-77 vs 103-105 |
| low | Stale cross-file comments about subscript(safe:): RouteSimulator.swift:156-159 says it lives in Engine/RoutineManager.swift (true, line 471) while RoutineStudioView.swift:207 says it was moved to Engine/Route/RouteSimulator.swift (false). One of the two comments will mislead whoever moves these files during the redesign. | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/Route/RouteSimulator.swift:156-159 and AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Views/RoutineStudioView.swift:207 |
| low | RouteSimulator.start() discards the SimulationSession it receives (`_ = session // captured for future use`, line 39), so the simulator has no session id to correlate telemetry or history with. Any redesign wanting per-run history for route simulations has to add that plumbing. | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/Route/RouteSimulator.swift:38-39 |
| low | MotionEngine.totalDistanceTravelled is accumulated and reset but has no accessor — genuinely unreachable state. Distance is instead recomputed by RouteSimulator. | low | SpeedProfile.speed(at:progress:) (Models/SimulationTypes.swift:158-171) is exercised only by AutoSpoofVNTests/SimulationTests.swift:66-69 — MotionEngine implements its own inline phase math and never calls it. Two divergent speed models exist; do not assume the tested one is the shipped one. | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Models/SimulationTypes.swift:158-171 vs AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/Motion/MotionEngine.swift:44-63 |
| low | HeadingEngine is a struct stored in `private var headingEngine` on a @MainActor class. Correct today, but if a refactor moves heading smoothing into a closure, a captured copy, or a `let`, the smoothing state silently stops accumulating with no compiler error. | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/Motion/HeadingEngine.swift:5 and AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/Route/RouteSimulator.swift:19 |
| low | HeadingEngine's doc comment claims it handles the dateline; there is no dateline-specific code. The underlying MotionEngine.bearing math does cross ±180 correctly, so behaviour is fine — but the comment overstates and could stop someone from testing that path. | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/Motion/HeadingEngine.swift:4 |
| low | ScenarioEngine's 1000-iteration loop cap and moveToward's 10000-iteration cap terminate silently with no published signal. A looping scenario simply stops with progress = 1.0 and no explanation. | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/Scenario/ScenarioEngine.swift:96 and 180 |

Toàn bộ lịch tour (ngủ/ăn/tham quan) lệch giờ trên thiết bị khác múi giờ. | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/FlightManager.swift:376-379 |

### Engines: DeviceManager + DeviceTransport + SelfPairingManager + ConnectionRecovery + CoordinateServer + ShadowrocketManager

| Muc | Rui ro | Vi tri |
|---|---|---|
| medium | generateDiagnostics() is called from inside SwiftUI view bodies. DeviceManagerView.swift:100 evaluates it on every body pass, and the two DiagnosticsV2View exporters call it again. It reaches into SpoofEngine, SelfPairingManager and BackgroundKeeper singletons and does a FileManager.fileExists (via hasSavedPairing) synchronously on the main thread during layout. | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Views/DeviceManagerView.swift:100; AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/Device/DeviceManager.swift:113-128 |
| medium | hasSavedPairing is a filesystem probe, not a @Published value. Views that show pairing availability (DeviceManagerView, DiagnosticsV2View, the health row) will not refresh when pairing succeeds or the file is deleted — they only update when something else forces a re-render. | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/SelfPairingManager.swift:75-77 |
| medium | Timers owned by never-deinited singletons. ShadowrocketManager's 5s vpnCheckTimer (ShadowrocketManager.swift:188) is only invalidated in deinit, which never runs for a .shared singleton — it polls getifaddrs every 5 seconds for the life of the process, including in the background. DeviceManager's 20s heartbeat has the same shape but at least has an explicit stop path. | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/ShadowrocketManager.swift:187-193, 251-253 |
| medium | isVPNActive is a one-way latch on one signal and a flapping poll on the other. observeCoordinateServer sets isVPNActive = true when requestCount increases and never sets it false (ShadowrocketManager.swift:174-178), while checkVPNStatus flips it in both directions from any utun/ipsec/ppp interface — which is true for ANY VPN, not just Shadowrocket. The banner's 'ready' state is therefore not trustworthy. | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/ShadowrocketManager.swift:169-181, 195-219 |
| medium | ConnectionRecovery's retry loop mutates @MainActor state from inside a detached Task without an explicit hop and reads self.isRecovering as its loop condition; combined with the 3s post-connect sleep and the 5-step backoff the worst case is ~62s during which nothing in the UI shows progress and there is no reachable cancel button. | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/Device/ConnectionRecovery.swift:91-117 (cancelRecovery at :119 has no caller) |
| medium | SelfPairingManager.cancel() cannot actually stop the running Rust pairing host — it only stops the Bonjour NetService and resets the UI phase. The 180s FFI call keeps running on a global queue and will later post its own DispatchQueue.main.async result, which can overwrite the .idle phase the user just chose. | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/SelfPairingManager.swift:101-104 vs :121-169 |
| medium | requestLocalNetworkAccess() always resumes true — every NWBrowser state branch and the 1.5s fallback timer call resumeOnce(true). The .failed("Cần cấp quyền Mạng cục bộ...") path in startAutoPairing is unreachable, so a user who denied Local Network permission gets a confusing FFI-level failure instead of the actionable message. | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/SelfPairingManager.swift:228-265 (unreachable branch at :93-96) |
| medium | CoordinateServer.start() swallows the bind failure. If port 8765 is already taken the do/catch only logs, isRunning stays false, and the UI shows 'CoordinateServer tắt' with no reason. There is no error surface for the new UI to render. | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/CoordinateServer.swift:66-68 |
| medium | Device Manager and pairing are only reachable while the simulation is idle. QuickActionsBar (which carries onDevices) is rendered under `if !coordinator.state.isActive` (MainViewV2.swift:67-77), so a user whose device drops mid-session has no in-app route to the connect/pair controls. | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Views/MainViewV2.swift:67-77, 116 |
| low | Diagnostics export writes silently to the Documents directory with no share sheet, no confirmation, and no error surface (`try?` twice). Users have no way to retrieve the file unless the app declares UIFileSharingEnabled. | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Views/DiagnosticsV2View.swift:146-181 |
| low | DeviceManager.transportType is a hardcoded "DVT" string that is displayed as fact in three places and baked into connectionState = .connected(transport:), even when SpoofEngine actually connected over RPPairing at 10.7.0.1:49152. The real transport kind is computed in SpoofEngine.connectLoopback and DVTDeviceTransport but never propagated. | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/Device/DeviceManager.swift:14, 165; AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/SpoofEngine.swift:269-289 |
| low | DeviceManager.iosVersion is published and never assigned — it is permanently the empty string. Any new UI that renders it will show a blank field. | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/Device/DeviceManager.swift:12 |
| medium | DeviceManager.clearSimulation() and SpoofEngine.clearSimulation() have the same name but opposite semantics (the former does NOT halt, the latter sets isHalted and blocks all further writes). Easy to mis-wire the 'Xoá GPS' button during a rewrite and permanently freeze the simulation. | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/Device/DeviceManager.swift:61-63 vs AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/SpoofEngine.swift:317-328 |
| medium | The whole cluster is singleton-driven: DeviceManager.shared, SelfPairingManager.shared, ConnectionRecovery.shared, CoordinateServer.shared and ShadowrocketManager.shared are all reached statically from views, from SimulationCoordinator, from TimerAudit and from the App struct. Nothing is injectable and nothing is testable — AutoSpoofVNTests references none of these six files. | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Views/*.swift, AutoSpoofVN/Sources/AutoSpoofVNApp.swift:78-107, AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/TimerAudit.swift:50-51 |

### Engines: BackgroundKeeper + LiveActivityManager + AppRecoveryManager + TimerAudit + DeterministicRandom + AppLogger

| Muc | Rui ro | Vi tri |
|---|---|---|
| medium | The Live Activity update stream includes SpoofEngine.$currentCoordinate, which changes at up to 10 Hz during route simulation. Throttled to 800 ms it still produces ~4500 ActivityKit updates per hour, indefinitely, with no stop condition. ActivityKit budgets updates and iOS may throttle or kill the activity; each update is an unstructured Task { await activity.update(...) } so ordering is not guaranteed and updates can land out of order. | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/LiveActivityManager.swift:83-86, 112-124 |
| medium | SystemHealth.background, .simulation, .routing and .liveActivity are declared but NEVER assigned — only health.device and health.transport are written (SimulationCoordinator.swift:345-360). Because health.overall requires allSatisfy(.healthy), the overall system-health indicator can literally never report healthy. Any redesign that surfaces a green/amber/red health chip will show a permanently non-green state. | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Models/SimulationTypes.swift:308-320 vs. Coordinator/SimulationCoordinator.swift:345-360 |
| low | AppRecoveryManager.shared is instantiated as an @StateObject in the App struct (AutoSpoofVNApp.swift:22), and its private init() calls checkForPendingRecovery() which can set hasPendingRecovery = true. So merely constructing the App can raise the alert — including over OnboardingViewV2, before the user has completed onboarding. | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/AppRecoveryManager.swift:28-30 + AutoSpoofVNApp.swift:22, 52 |
| medium | checkpoint() only fires on willResignActive. A hard crash while the app is in the foreground (never resigning active) leaves no checkpoint at all, and a checkpoint written on resign-active is not refreshed while the app runs in the background for hours — so a background kill recovers to an hour-stale coordinate and the elapsed/speed fields are meaningless. | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/AutoSpoofVNApp.swift:62-64 + Engine/AppRecoveryManager.swift:33-53 |
| low | checkForPendingRecovery() runs ONLY in init. If the app is backgrounded, killed and relaunched into the same process-less state it works, but a resumed process (didBecomeActive after a long background) never re-checks. There is no public API to re-scan, so a redesigned UI cannot offer a 'check for recoverable session' action without adding one. | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/AppRecoveryManager.swift:28-30, 73-83 |
| medium | SettingsView reads BackgroundKeeper.shared.isAudioRunning WITHOUT holding it as an @StateObject/@ObservedObject, so the 'Đang chạy/Tắt' badge is a snapshot that never refreshes while the sheet is open. Easy to replicate accidentally in the new UI. | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Views/SettingsView.swift:86-87 |
| low | DiagnosticsV2View wraps itself in its own NavigationStack with a 'Đóng' toolbar button that calls dismiss() (DiagnosticsV2View.swift:13-115). It is presented BOTH as a sheet (MainViewV2.swift:120) and pushed via NavigationLink (SettingsView.swift:134) — the pushed case nests a NavigationStack inside a NavigationStack and shows a redundant 'Đóng' button. | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Views/DiagnosticsV2View.swift:13-115 |
| medium | Diagnostics export writes to the app Documents directory with no share sheet, no confirmation and no error handling (try? both times). Info.plist declares neither UIFileSharingEnabled nor LSSupportsOpeningDocumentsInPlace, so the exported file is unreachable by the user — the feature appears to work and produces nothing usable. | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Views/DiagnosticsV2View.swift:146-183; AutoSpoofVN/Resources/Info.plist |
| medium | BackgroundKeeper.enableBackgroundLocation() sets allowsBackgroundLocationUpdates = true, which throws an ObjC exception (uncatchable in Swift) if UIBackgroundModes loses the 'location' entry. Info.plist currently has both 'audio' and 'location' (Info.plist UIBackgroundModes) — a redesign or Info.plist cleanup that removes 'location' will crash the app on permission grant. | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/BackgroundKeeper.swift:195-202 + AutoSpoofVN/Resources/Info.plist UIBackgroundModes |
| medium | start() returns isAudioRunning synchronously but startAudio() is asynchronous, so the return value is always the previous (usually false) state. SpoofEngine.startBackgroundKeepAlive() similarly returns isKeepAliveRunning immediately after calling start() (SpoofEngine.swift:216, 230), so any UI that trusts this Bool to mean 'keep-alive succeeded' will show a false negative. | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/BackgroundKeeper.swift:59-65 + Engine/SpoofEngine.swift:214-231 |
| medium | Neither BackgroundKeeper nor LiveActivityManager is exercised by AutoSpoofVNTests — only SpoofEngine.makeSilentWavData (the WAV bytes) is tested (EngineTests.swift:9-59). There is no regression net for the background/Live Activity behaviour a redesign might disturb. | D:/ToolGN/AutoSpoofVN/AutoSpoofVNTests/EngineTests.swift |
| low | DeviceManager.generateDiagnostics() hardcodes liveActivityEnabled: true instead of reading ActivityAuthorizationInfo().areActivitiesEnabled — the exported diagnostics will claim Live Activities are enabled even when the user has turned them off in Settings. | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/Device/DeviceManager.swift:126 |
| low | runDiagnostics() emits a hardcoded placeholder row ('Live Activity', .unknown, 'Kiểm tra ActivityKit') that performs no check at all. A redesigned diagnostics screen inheriting this will display a permanently-unknown row. | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Coordinator/SimulationCoordinator.swift:401-406 |
| low | AppLogger uses os.Logger; non-literal String interpolations default to .private and are redacted as <private> in Release builds. Several diagnostics-critical logs interpolate dynamic values (AppRecoveryManager.swift:64 coordinates, BackgroundKeeper.swift:225 error text, LiveActivityManager.swift:97 error) and will therefore be unreadable in a shipped build. | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/AppLogger.swift:6-16 and its call sites |
| medium | TimerAudit.report() rows report proxy signals rather than real timer liveness — e.g. the SpoofEngine row uses isSimulating even though its 20 s heartBeatTimer is created by startBackgroundKeepAlive() and never invalidated. If the redesign surfaces this table in Diagnostics it will display misleading data. | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/TimerAudit.swift:43-53 vs. Engine/SpoofEngine.swift:214-237 |
| medium | TimerAudit's doc-comment table (lines 6-32) claims RouteSimulator/ScenarioEngine/ReplayEngine/RoutineManager-movement timers exist in the inventory and asserts 'Không có timer nào chạy orphan' — but report() covers only 7 of the 13 listed, and stopBackgroundKeepAlive() is never called, so the claim is factually false. Do not use this comment as an architecture reference during the redesign. | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/TimerAudit.swift:6-32 |
| low | DeterministicRandom.nextInt(in:) uses `next() % span`, which is modulo-biased, and the header comment calls the algorithm 'xoshiro64*' when it is SplitMix64. Harmless today (no callers) but misleading if the redesign wires it into GPS noise for QA reproducibility. | AutoSpoofVN/Sources/Engine/DeterministicRandom.swift:12, 30-34 |
